That passage of time is more than sentimental to me. It is evidence that seasons move whether we are ready or not.

<a id="BOOK-B2718"></a>
My Stewardship Blueprint did not appear in that hospital room. It began there as a question, and it has been taking shape ever since. There have been moments of clarity, conviction, emotional growth, and spiritual renewal. There have also been long stretches when I could not see much progress and wondered whether I was moving forward at all.

<a id="BOOK-B2719"></a>
Looking back, I can see that God was still working in those quieter seasons. He was teaching me lessons I was not yet ready to name, placing people in my life who would shape me, allowing responsibility to mature me, exposing the limits of old scoreboards, and preparing me for assignments I could not yet see.

<a id="BOOK-B2720"></a>
The process has not been linear, and it is not complete. I am still learning to be more present with Kristi and our kids. I am still learning what to build, what to delegate, what to protect, and what belongs in another season. I am still learning to steward my body and energy, define enough, use freedom for generosity, mentor and multiply rather than merely produce, and trust God with outcomes I cannot control.

<a id="BOOK-B2721"></a>
That is why my Blueprint is not a monument to a finished life. It is a guide for a life still being formed.

<a id="BOOK-B2722"></a>
The same will be true for you. There will be moments when direction becomes clear and seasons when the next step is all you can see. There will be commitments you keep, rhythms you lose, Guardrails that protect you, decisions you regret, relationships that deepen, and course corrections that become part of your story. Some changes will happen quickly. Others will take years.

<a id="BOOK-B2723"></a>
Do not despise the small step.

<a id="BOOK-B2724"></a>
A meaningful life is rarely built through one dramatic decision. It is built through a lifetime of intentional ones: one honest conversation, one protected relationship, one act of obedience, one financial choice, one renewed rhythm, one apology, one boundary, one generous response, and one decision to return when drift begins.

<a id="BOOK-B2725"></a>
Then another.

<a id="BOOK-B2726"></a>
Building my Stewardship Blueprint has become a lifetime journey of moving forward with intention, stewarding what God has entrusted to me, learning to live a life increasingly marked by love, and multiplying whatever good He chooses to do through it.

<a id="BOOK-B2727"></a>
That is the invitation now in front of you.

<a id="BOOK-B2728"></a>
You do not need to know the entire path, and you do not need to become a finished product. You need enough clarity to take the next faithful step and enough humility to keep returning when the way becomes unclear.

<a id="BOOK-B2729"></a>
The life you are building is already under construction. Ask what faithfulness requires in this season. Take the next step, and then take one more. Over a lifetime, those intentional acts of stewardship can become a life full of love, a legacy multiplied through others, and a life that truly matters.

<a id="BOOK-B2730"></a>
With whatever God has entrusted to you, build accordingly.


<a id="BOOK-B2731"></a>

<a id="BOOK-B2744"></a>
### About the Author

<a id="BOOK-B2745"></a>
Michael J. Gauthier is a financial advisor, business owner, author, and creator of the Stewardship Blueprint. For more than two decades, he has helped individuals and families think beyond accumulation and align their resources with purpose, generosity, freedom, and legacy. He founded Strategic Income Group and writes from the intersection of Christian faith, whole-life stewardship, leadership, family, health, money, and impact. Michael lives in Arizona with his wife, Kristi, and their children, Colton, Brooklyn, and Ashlyn.

<a id="STUDY5-B0016"></a>
### A Word Before You Begin

<a id="STUDY5-B0017"></a>
Welcome. I’m glad this guide is in your hands.

<a id="STUDY5-B0018"></a>
You may have picked this up on your own, or a friend or leader may have handed it to you. However you arrived, the invitation is the same: for the next six weeks, take an honest look at the life you are actually building — and build it more on purpose.

<a id="STUDY5-B0019"></a>
The Stewardship Blueprint is built around one central conviction: your life is not merely something you own. It is something entrusted to you. Your faith, relationships, body, time, opportunities, money, energy, influence, and future impact all deserve intentional stewardship.

<a id="STUDY5-B0020"></a>
This isn’t a book you read alone and set down. It’s a structure you build together with a small group of men, one layer each week, holding each other to what you say you’ll do. The teaching gives you a map; the men beside you give you the resolve to follow it. You bring the honesty.

<a id="STUDY5-B0021"></a>
|  | Confidentiality is the foundation.<br>You are free to share as much or as little as you choose, and nothing is required of you that you are not ready to share. What is said in the group stays in the group. Trust is what makes honest change possible, so guard it for one another. |
| --- | --- |

<a id="STUDY5-B0022"></a>
You do not need to redesign your entire life in six weeks. You simply need enough honesty to see clearly, enough courage to take one faithful step, and enough humility to let other men walk with you. That’s where a building a life that matters gets built.

<a id="STUDY5-B0023"></a>
Let’s build.

<a id="STUDY5-B0024"></a>
— Michael J. Gauthier

<a id="STUDY5-B0025"></a>
Author, The Life You’re Building  |  Creator of the Stewardship Blueprint

<a id="STUDY5-B0026"></a>
### Before We Begin

<a id="STUDY5-B0027"></a>
##### What this group is

<a id="STUDY5-B0028"></a>
A space for honest reflection, practical action, and prayer.

<a id="STUDY5-B0029"></a>
A guided journey through faith and identity, purpose, relationships, health, joy, finances, guardrails, rhythms, energy, and legacy.

<a id="STUDY5-B0030"></a>
A place to build real accountability — where men keep each other moving between meetings, not just during them.

<a id="STUDY5-B0031"></a>
##### What this group is not

<a id="STUDY5-B0032"></a>
A performance contest, a financial sales event, or a place to impress one another.

<a id="STUDY5-B0033"></a>
Professional therapy, medical care, legal advice, or individualized financial advice.

<a id="STUDY5-B0034"></a>
A demand that you disclose anything you are not ready to share.

<a id="STUDY5-B0035"></a>
##### How each meeting works

<a id="STUDY5-B0036"></a>
| Before we meet | 15–20 minutes of reading and reflection on your own. Arrive ready to share one insight. |
| --- | --- |
| Personal check-in | A brief, honest opening round. Each man in a sentence or two. |
| Watch &amp; teaching | The group watches the short weekly video together, then a brief teaching opens the layer. |
| Discussion | The heart of the meeting. Honest conversation around two or three questions. |
| This week’s action | Each man names one concrete step before the next meeting. |
| Prayer | We close by praying for one another. |

<a id="STUDY5-B0037"></a>
#### Group Covenant

<a id="STUDY5-B0038"></a>
For the next six weeks, I commit to:

<a id="STUDY5-B0039"></a>
Show up. Make the six weeks a real commitment, and communicate when I cannot attend.

<a id="STUDY5-B0040"></a>
Prepare. Complete the short pre-work and arrive ready to share one insight.

<a id="STUDY5-B0041"></a>
Be honest. Speak from my own experience rather than giving polished answers.

<a id="STUDY5-B0042"></a>
Listen well. Ask questions before giving advice. I will not try to fix another man.

<a id="STUDY5-B0043"></a>
Respect confidentiality. Share my own story outside the group, never another man’s.

<a id="STUDY5-B0044"></a>
Allow a pass. No one is required to answer every question.

<a id="STUDY5-B0045"></a>
Take action. End each week with one concrete step, not only a good conversation.

<a id="STUDY5-B0046"></a>
	Signature  	

<a id="STUDY5-B0047"></a>
	Date  	

<a id="STUDY5-B0048"></a>
### The Stewardship Blueprint

<a id="STUDY5-B0049"></a>
This image is a visual framework, not a formula for perfection. It helps us see that the different parts of life are connected — and that visible outcomes rest on deeper foundations.

<a id="STUDY5-B0050"></a>
*[Visual object in original source. See preserved original DOCX and extracted reference assets; not a newly approved diagram.]*

<a id="STUDY5-B0051"></a>
Bedrock.  Faith & Identity. What my life is rooted in, and where I receive worth.

<a id="STUDY5-B0052"></a>
Foundation.  Mission, Values, Life Verse, Daily Purpose. What my life is for.

<a id="STUDY5-B0053"></a>
Four Pillars.  Family, Fitness, Fun, Finances. The everyday areas I am called to steward.

<a id="STUDY5-B0054"></a>
Guardrails.  Pre-decided boundaries that protect what matters before regret arrives.

<a id="STUDY5-B0055"></a>
Keystone Rhythms.  Small repeated rhythms that pull the rest of life into alignment.

<a id="STUDY5-B0056"></a>
Energy.  Physical, emotional, mental, and spiritual fuel — stewarded and renewed.

<a id="STUDY5-B0057"></a>
Legacy.  What my life multiplies in people and causes while I am still living.

<a id="STUDY5-B0058"></a>
How to read the blueprint. The goal is not perfect balance. Different seasons require different emphasis. The goal is faithful alignment: no important part of the structure is ignored indefinitely, and the whole life is directed toward what matters most.

<a id="STUDY5-B0059"></a>
##### Six principles beneath the diagram

<a id="STUDY5-B0060"></a>
Purpose anchors life — without it, the urgent always defeats the important.

<a id="STUDY5-B0061"></a>
Faith integrates life — what I believe should shape what I decide.

<a id="STUDY5-B0062"></a>
Stewardship structures life — entrusted things require intentional management.

<a id="STUDY5-B0063"></a>
Guardrails protect life — drift is real, and regret is often preventable.

<a id="STUDY5-B0064"></a>
Rhythms strengthen life — repeated faithfulness becomes the architecture of character.

<a id="STUDY5-B0065"></a>
Energy sustains life — I cannot pour out forever without renewal.

<a id="STUDY5-B0066"></a>
### The Six-Week Journey

<a id="STUDY5-B0067"></a>
| Week 1 | Wake Up | Name current reality and notice drift. |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Week 2 | See the Blueprint | Clarify bedrock, identity, values, mission, and daily purpose. |
| Week 3 | Evaluate the Pillars | Assess family, fitness, fun, and finances; choose one priority. |
| Week 4 | Install the Guardrails | Build specific boundaries that protect what matters most. |
| Week 5 | Strengthen the Structure | Design keystone rhythms and steward energy. |
| Week 6 | Design My Life | Assemble a Personal Blueprint, name a legacy, commit to 30 days. |

<a id="STUDY5-B0068"></a>
|  | One central question runs through all six weeks:<br>What kind of life am I actually building? |
| --- | --- |

<a id="STUDY5-B0069"></a>
### The Created for More Check-In

<a id="STUDY5-B0070"></a>
A Stewardship Blueprint Assessment

<a id="STUDY5-B0071"></a>
This assessment walks through the full seven-layer Blueprint, because a life is not built only on family, fitness, fun, and finances. It is built from the ground up: on what you believe, what you are building toward, the relationships and resources entrusted to you, the boundaries that protect you, the rhythms that shape you, the energy that fuels you, and the legacy your life is multiplying. Each layer is scored out of 20, for a total Blueprint Alignment Score out of 140.

<a id="STUDY5-B0072"></a>
Recommended online option. Take the Created for More Check-In at michaeljgauthier.com/created-for-more-check-in for automated scoring and progress tracking. You may also complete the assessment in this workbook and score it yourself.

<a id="STUDY5-B0073"></a>
Complete the BEFORE assessment before Week 1. At the end of Week 6, retake it online or complete the AFTER column. One month later, retake it online or complete the 30-DAY column. Each week of this study builds one of these layers—so you can see where progress is happening.

<a id="STUDY5-B0074"></a>
|  | Rate each statement 1 to 5:<br>1 rarely true    •    2 occasionally    •    3 somewhat / room to grow    •    4 mostly true    •    5 consistently true and intentionally protected |
| --- | --- |

<a id="STUDY5-B0075"></a>
The value is not in the score alone. It is in the honesty.  A lower score after reflection does not always mean failure; sometimes greater honesty produces a more accurate rating. Compare the numbers with the changes you actually made and the stories you can tell.

<a id="STUDY5-B0076"></a>
| Statement  (rate 1–5) | Before | After | 30-Day |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Layer 1  —  Bedrock — Faith &amp; Identity<br>Is my identity rooted in something deeper than performance and pressure? |  |  |  |
| I know who I am beneath my roles, titles, and achievements. |  |  |  |
| My daily choices flow from a settled identity, not from proving myself. |  |  |  |
| I return to my faith as a source of stability, not just a Sunday habit. |  |  |  |

<a id="STUDY5-B0099"></a>

<a id="STUDY5-B0107"></a>
| WEEK 1<br>Wake Up<br>What kind of life am I building? |
| --- |

<a id="STUDY5-B0108"></a>
#### Read Before We Meet

<a id="STUDY5-B0109"></a>
Most men do not wake up and decide to neglect what matters. Drift usually happens quietly. One full week becomes a full season. One postponed conversation becomes distance. One busy month becomes a life organized around urgency. Nothing feels dramatic in the moment, yet something is being built.

<a id="STUDY5-B0110"></a>
Busyness can disguise drift because activity feels responsible. We answer messages, solve problems, provide, lead, and keep moving. The danger is not always laziness. The danger is motion without direction. We can be productive and still be misaligned. We can succeed according to a scoreboard that is too small.

<a id="STUDY5-B0111"></a>
Stewardship begins with a different assumption: my life is entrusted, not merely owned. My days, relationships, body, opportunities, resources, influence, and energy are gifts for which I carry responsibility. That does not mean I control every outcome. It means I am called to pay attention and respond faithfully.

<a id="STUDY5-B0112"></a>
The first step is not to fix everything. The first step is to see clearly. Psalm 90:12 (NIV) is a prayer for exactly this kind of clarity. Awareness is not condemnation. It is grace arriving before regret. It gives us the chance to notice where our stated priorities and our lived priorities have separated.

<a id="STUDY5-B0113"></a>
Your calendar, spending, rhythms, screen time, recurring thoughts, and emotional energy often tell the truth before your intentions do. This week, resist the urge to give an impressive answer. Describe the life that is actually being built. Notice what is aligned. Notice what is strained. Notice what you keep postponing.

<a id="STUDY5-B0114"></a>
|  | THE BIG IDEA<br>Drift is subtle. Design is intentional. The life I am building is revealed by my calendar, attention, and energy — not only my intentions. |

Notes

<a id="STUDY5-B0133"></a>
| WEEK 2<br>See the Blueprint<br>Bedrock &amp; Foundation |

<a id="STUDY5-B0134"></a>
#### Read Before We Meet

<a id="STUDY5-B0135"></a>
A blueprint can show where the walls go, but it cannot tell you why the house is being built. That question comes first. A disciplined life can still be aimed at the wrong definition of success if the deeper foundation is never examined.

<a id="STUDY5-B0136"></a>
In the Stewardship Blueprint, Bedrock is faith and identity. It asks: What is my life rooted in? Where do I receive worth? What remains true when performance changes, people are disappointed, or plans fall apart? If identity is built on approval, achievement, money, control, or image, pressure will eventually expose the weakness. For a follower of Jesus, identity is received before it is achieved. We work from worth, not for worth.

<a id="STUDY5-B0137"></a>
Faith is not one room in the house. It is the integrating center of the whole structure. It should shape how we love, lead, work, spend, rest, respond, and define success. Compartmentalized faith says, “I believe.” Integrated faith asks, “What is that belief producing in my actual life?”

<a id="STUDY5-B0138"></a>
Foundation answers a second question: What is my life for? In this guide it has four parts. Mission names the assignment or contribution that matters in this season. Values are priorities you are willing to protect with your calendar, money, and decisions. A life verse or guiding Scripture gives language to the truth you want to remember. Daily purpose translates the big picture into the way you want to show up today.

<a id="STUDY5-B0139"></a>
These statements do not need to be perfect. A rough, honest mission is more useful than a polished sentence that does not move you. Values are not words you admire; they are filters that help you say yes and no. Identity comes before strategy. When bedrock and foundation become clearer, choices do not always become easy, but they become more aligned.

<a id="STUDY5-B0140"></a>
|  | THE BIG IDEA<br>Identity before strategy. When I know who I am and what my life is for, every open door stops looking like my door. |

<a id="STUDY5-B0158"></a>
Notes

<a id="STUDY5-B0159"></a>
| WEEK 3<br>Evaluate the Pillars<br>Family, Fitness, Fun &amp; Finances |
| --- |

<a id="STUDY5-B0160"></a>
#### Read Before We Meet

<a id="STUDY5-B0161"></a>
The four pillars represent everyday areas of responsibility that support the life we are building. They are connected, and each carries weight.

<a id="STUDY5-B0162"></a>
Family includes marriage, parenting, friendship, mentoring, and community — the people entrusted to our relational influence. It asks whether the people closest to us receive intentional presence or only what remains after work and responsibility.

<a id="STUDY5-B0163"></a>
Fitness is the stewardship of the body that carries the calling: movement, strength, sleep, nutrition, recovery, medical wisdom, and the physical capacity to love, serve, work, and enjoy life. Health is not ultimate, but neglect in the body eventually affects patience, energy, relationships, finances, and opportunities.

<a id="STUDY5-B0164"></a>
Fun includes joy, rest, adventure, celebration, play, Sabbath, and meaningful experiences. It is not shallow escape. It is the intentional reception of life-giving gifts that renew the soul and create memory. Responsible men often postpone joy until life slows down; life rarely slows down on its own.

<a id="STUDY5-B0165"></a>
Finances include earning, spending, saving, giving, planning, freedom, provision, and impact. Money is a powerful tool but a poor identity. It often reveals what we trust, fear, value, and prioritize. Financial stewardship is not only about having more; it is about aligning resources with purpose.

<a id="STUDY5-B0166"></a>
The goal is not equal time in all four areas every week. Seasons change. The danger comes when temporary imbalance becomes permanent neglect. This week, evaluate without exaggerating and without minimizing. Name one win and one concern in each pillar. Then choose the one pillar that most needs a focused next step. Strengthening one load-bearing area can create a positive ripple through the whole structure.

<a id="STUDY5-B0167"></a>
|  | THE BIG IDEA<br>The goal is not perfect balance. It is faithful stewardship — making sure no load-bearing pillar is neglected indefinitely. |

<a id="STUDY5-B0180"></a>
Notes

<a id="STUDY5-B0181"></a>
| WEEK 4<br>Install the Guardrails<br>Protect what matters |
| --- |

<a id="STUDY5-B0182"></a>
#### Read Before We Meet

<a id="STUDY5-B0183"></a>
Roadside guardrails are not placed in the middle of the danger. They are placed before it. They do not prevent every collision, but they keep a moment of drift from becoming a catastrophe.

<a id="STUDY5-B0184"></a>
Personal guardrails work the same way. They are pre-decided standards or boundaries that keep us away from places where wisdom becomes harder and regret becomes more likely. Good intentions are not guardrails. “I should be more careful” is not specific enough to protect anything under pressure.

<a id="STUDY5-B0185"></a>
Guardrails may be needed around time, technology, money, relationships, sexuality, work, alcohol, health, travel, emotional reactions, or spiritual life. The right guardrail is personal because the danger zone is personal. A boundary that is unnecessary for one man may be wise for another.

<a id="STUDY5-B0186"></a>
Guardrails are not a sign of weakness. They are a form of stewardship. They protect freedom, trust, energy, and future options. A work cutoff can protect marriage and sleep. A spending rule can protect generosity and peace. A technology boundary can protect attention. A health guardrail can keep one difficult week from becoming a destructive season.

<a id="STUDY5-B0187"></a>
A useful guardrail has four parts. First, name what matters — what are you protecting? Second, name the danger or trigger — when are you most likely to drift? Third, make the boundary concrete — what will you do or not do? Fourth, tell someone — accountability makes the decision real before pressure arrives. Wise boundaries and honest heart work belong together; ask what desire, fear, resentment, comparison, or need for approval is underneath the behavior.

<a id="STUDY5-B0188"></a>
|  | THE BIG IDEA<br>A guardrail is a decision made before the moment of pressure. It is not restriction for its own sake — it protects something I do not want to lose. |
| --- | --- |
| Check-In layer | Layer 4 — Guardrails. A low score here is exactly what this week is built to raise. |
| This week’s outcome | One specific, measurable guardrail — written down and shared with one trusted person. |
|  | The prudent see danger and take refuge, but the simple keep going and pay the penalty.<br>— Proverbs 27:12 (NIV)<br>So, if you think you are standing firm, be careful that you don’t fall!<br>— 1 Corinthians 10:12 (NIV) |
| Before We Meet — Pre-Work |  |

<a id="STUDY5-B0189"></a>
Look for places where consequences are predictable, temptation is recurring, or the cost of drift would be high.

<a id="STUDY5-B0190"></a>
Where are you most vulnerable to drift right now? Name two or three areas.

<a id="STUDY5-B0191"></a>
Recall a regret — yours or one you watched up close — that a guardrail set earlier might have prevented.

<a id="STUDY5-B0192"></a>
Draft one guardrail using the four parts: what it protects, the trigger, the specific boundary, and who you’ll tell.

<a id="STUDY5-B0193"></a>
##### Guardrail design test

<a id="STUDY5-B0194"></a>
Is it specific enough that I will know whether I kept it?

<a id="STUDY5-B0195"></a>
Does it begin before the danger zone rather than inside it?

<a id="STUDY5-B0196"></a>
Does it protect something I deeply value?

<a id="STUDY5-B0197"></a>
Have I told someone who can ask me about it?

| WEEK 5<br>Strengthen the Structure<br>Keystone Rythms &amp; Energy |
| --- |

<a id="STUDY5-B0208"></a>
#### Read Before We Meet

<a id="STUDY5-B0209"></a>
Guardrails keep us away from predictable danger. Rythms help us move repeatedly toward what matters. The life we build will be shaped less by occasional inspiration than by recurring rhythms.

<a id="STUDY5-B0210"></a>
A keystone habit is a small routine that creates a larger ripple. A morning walk may improve sleep, mood, food choices, prayer, and patience. A weekly planning conversation may improve marriage, family logistics, money, and rest. A phone-free dinner may strengthen attention, conversation, and family culture. The best habit is not the most impressive one. It is the one that is aligned, repeatable, and likely to strengthen several parts of the blueprint.

<a id="STUDY5-B0211"></a>
Start with identity: What kind of man am I becoming? Then choose a behavior that proves that identity in a small way. Make the habit specific — name the cue, the minimum version, the normal version, the environment, and how you will track it. A minimum version protects continuity on hard days. Missing once is information; allowing one miss to become a new pattern is the danger. Recovery is part of the system.

<a id="STUDY5-B0212"></a>
Rythms require energy, and energy must also be stewarded. Physical energy supports strength and endurance. Emotional energy shapes the tone we bring to relationships. Mental energy affects attention, judgment, and creativity. Spiritual energy connects us to God, identity, hope, and purpose.

<a id="STUDY5-B0213"></a>
Energy is renewable, but not unlimited. Healthy life has a pulse: investment and renewal, exertion and recovery, work and Sabbath. Continuous output without renewal becomes debt. This week, notice what drains and restores you. Some draining responsibilities cannot be removed, but they can be redesigned, shared, bounded, or followed by renewal. Choose one keystone habit and one renewal rhythm — small enough to practice and clear enough to measure.

<a id="STUDY5-B0214"></a>
|  | THE BIG IDEA<br>I am shaped less by occasional inspiration than by recurring rhythms. One keystone habit, and one rhythm of renewal, can strengthen the whole structure. |

<a id="STUDY5-B0234"></a>
#### Read Before We Meet

<a id="STUDY5-B0235"></a>
A blueprint is valuable only if it guides the building. Insight without action can become another form of delay. The purpose of these six weeks is not to produce a perfect document. It is to create enough clarity to live differently.

<a id="STUDY5-B0236"></a>
Your Personal Blueprint brings the parts together. Bedrock names the faith and identity from which you want to live. Foundation names mission, values, Scripture, and daily purpose. The pillars reveal where attention is needed. Guardrails protect what matters. Keystone rythms create repeated alignment. An energy plan helps sustain the work. Legacy names what you want your life to multiply in people and causes.

<a id="STUDY5-B0237"></a>
Legacy is not only what is left after death. It is what other people experience from your life now. It is the culture of your home, the character you model, the stories you tell, the people you mentor, the generosity you practice, the courage you lend, and the faith you pass forward. Financial inheritance may be part of legacy, but relational and spiritual formation begin much earlier.

<a id="STUDY5-B0238"></a>
Your Blueprint should be specific enough to guide a decision and flexible enough to grow with your season. It is a living document, not a contract with perfection. Choose a 30-day commitment — long enough to create evidence and short enough to feel real. Decide what you will start, stop, continue, protect, and discuss. Choose an accountability person and a review date. Then share the Blueprint with someone close to you.

<a id="STUDY5-B0239"></a>
The most important outcome is not the page you complete. It is the man you are becoming and the life other people experience because you chose to stop drifting. Build with humility. Review with honesty. Begin with one faithful next step.

<a id="STUDY5-B0240"></a>
|  | THE BIG IDEA<br>The most important outcome is not the page I complete. It is the man I am becoming — and the life others experience because I chose to stop drifting. |

<a id="STUDY5-B0249"></a>
### My Personal Blueprint

<a id="STUDY5-B0250"></a>
Use these pages to create a working Blueprint for this season. Keep the language clear and practical. The goal is not to predict every detail of the future. The goal is to make your next decisions from a wiser structure. Review it at 30 and 90 days; update it when your season changes, but do not change it simply because a commitment becomes uncomfortable.

<a id="STUDY5-B0251"></a>

<a id="STUDY5-B0252"></a>

<a id="STUDY5-B0253"></a>
##### 2.  Foundation

<a id="STUDY5-B0254"></a>

<a id="STUDY5-B0255"></a>
##### 3.  The Four Pillars

<a id="STUDY5-B0256"></a>

<a id="STUDY5-B0257"></a>

The boundaries I am choosing, and what each one protects.

<a id="STUDY5-B0259"></a>

<a id="STUDY5-B0260"></a>
The small rhythm that strengthens the most, with its cue and minimum version.

<a id="STUDY5-B0261"></a>
##### 6.  My Energy Plan

<a id="STUDY5-B0262"></a>
What renews me, what drains me, and the rhythm I will protect.

<a id="STUDY5-B0263"></a>

Michael J. Gauthier

<a id="FE-B0008"></a>
### This Guide Belongs To

<a id="FE-B0013"></a>
### A Word Before You Begin

<a id="FE-B0014"></a>
Most men do not lose their purpose all at once. They lose the energy to live it well. They may still love God, care about their families, and want their work to matter, while a depleted body, reactive heart, scattered mind, or dry spirit begins to shape what everyone around them experiences.

<a id="FE-B0015"></a>
Fully Engaged is a companion experience within The Life You're Building and the Stewardship Blueprint. It focuses on Energy: the capacity that helps you live every other layer of the Blueprint. Energy is not the Bedrock; faith and identity in Christ are. Energy is the entrusted capacity through which you live your purpose, invest in the Four Pillars, honor Guardrails, sustain Keystone Habits, and multiply a Legacy.

<a id="FE-B0016"></a>
Over six weeks, you will complete the Energy Audit, examine physical, emotional, mental, and spiritual energy, test practical rhythms, and build a 30-day Energy Stewardship Plan. This is not a competition, a productivity sprint, or permission to make comfort the goal. It is an invitation to become more available to God and the people and purposes entrusted to you.

<a id="FE-B0017"></a>
You do not need to solve everything. Bring honesty, take one faithful step each week, and let a group of men know what you intend to do.

<a id="FE-B0018"></a>
- Michael J. Gauthier
Author, The Life You're Building  |  Creator of the Stewardship Blueprint

<a id="FE-B0019"></a>
### Before We Begin

<a id="FE-B0020"></a>
##### What this group is

<a id="FE-B0021"></a>
A place for honest reflection, biblical truth, practical action, prayer, and accountability.

<a id="FE-B0022"></a>
A guided examination of physical, emotional, mental, and spiritual energy.

<a id="FE-B0023"></a>
A place to take one faithful step each week, not perform for other men.

<a id="FE-B0024"></a>
#### Group Covenant

<a id="FE-B0025"></a>
Show up and prepare; communicate when you cannot attend.

<a id="FE-B0026"></a>
Speak from your own experience; do not diagnose, fix, or preach at another man.

<a id="FE-B0027"></a>
Listen with curiosity and ask before offering advice.

<a id="FE-B0028"></a>
Protect confidentiality. Share your own story outside the group, never another man's.

<a id="FE-B0029"></a>
Allow a pass. No one is required to disclose what he is not ready to share.

<a id="FE-B0030"></a>
Take action. Keep each commitment small enough to do and meaningful enough to matter.

<a id="FE-B0031"></a>
Seek qualified help when needed. This study is not medical or mental-health care.

<a id="FE-B0032"></a>
Signature

<a id="FE-B0034"></a>
### The Stewardship Blueprint

<a id="FE-B0035"></a>
*[Visual object in original source. See preserved original DOCX and extracted reference assets; not a newly approved diagram.]*

<a id="FE-B0036"></a>
Energy sits within the roof of the Blueprint because it helps sustain everything beneath it. The goal is not perfect balance or constant intensity. The goal is faithful alignment and the capacity to love God, serve people, and carry your current calling well.

<a id="FE-B0037"></a>
| THE CENTRAL DISTINCTION<br>Faith and identity are the Bedrock. Energy is an entrusted capacity, not the source of your worth. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0038"></a>
### The Energy Stewardship Model

<a id="FE-B0039"></a>
Deposits.  renew capacity: sleep, prayer, movement, honest connection, focus, play, nourishment, and Sabbath.

<a id="FE-B0040"></a>
Meaningful investments.  spend energy on what matters: worship, family, service, calling, growth, and generosity.

<a id="FE-B0041"></a>
Necessary withdrawals.  are real demands of a season: caregiving, grief, deadlines, illness, and transition.

<a id="FE-B0042"></a>
Avoidable leaks.  consume energy without producing faithful fruit: fragmentation, numbing, secrecy, unresolved conflict, and unexamined overcommitment.

<a id="FE-B0043"></a>
Energy debt.  develops when withdrawals repeatedly exceed renewal and shortcuts become normal.

<a id="FE-B0044"></a>
### Baseline Energy Audit

<a id="FE-B0045"></a>
Complete The Energy Audit Check-In before Week 1. The score is a mirror, not a grade. Answer from current reality rather than aspiration, then repeat it in Week 6.

<a id="FE-B0046"></a>

| Physical |  |  |  |  |
| Emotional |  |  |  |  |
| Mental |  |  |  |  |
| Spiritual |  |  |  |  |
| TOTAL /100 |  |  |  |  |

<a id="FE-B0047"></a>
### My Starting Picture

<a id="FE-B0048"></a>

<a id="FE-B0049"></a>

<a id="FE-B0051"></a>
| WEEK 1  &#124;  ALL FOUR ENERGY SOURCES<br>Wake Up<br>Tell the truth about your tank |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0052"></a>
| THE BIG IDEA<br>You cannot steward energy you will not honestly examine. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0053"></a>
This Week's Outcome

<a id="FE-B0054"></a>
Complete the Energy Audit, name current reality, and identify one avoidable energy leak.

<a id="FE-B0055"></a>
Read Before We Meet

<a id="FE-B0056"></a>
Busyness can disguise depletion. Capable men often keep producing long after their internal reserves have fallen. The calendar may still look successful while patience, presence, clarity, and joy quietly decline. Scripture offers a better beginning: invite God to search you, tell the truth about your condition, and let awareness become an act of stewardship rather than an occasion for shame.

<a id="FE-B0057"></a>
Not every energy withdrawal is a problem. Loving a child, serving someone in need, doing meaningful work, training hard, grieving, or carrying a difficult season may leave you tired and still be deeply aligned. The important distinction is between a meaningful investment, a necessary withdrawal, and an avoidable leak. The goal is not to eliminate fatigue; it is to recognize what your expenditure is producing.

<a id="FE-B0058"></a>
This week, resist the urge to rebuild your entire life. Observe first. Your pace, calendar, screen use, recurring thoughts, relationships, and physical signals are evidence. Ask where your energy is being renewed, where it is faithfully invested, where the season requires a real withdrawal, and where it is escaping without producing faithful fruit.

<a id="FE-B0059"></a>
### Scripture for Week 1

<a id="FE-B0060"></a>
Read each passage slowly. Notice what it reveals about God, human limits, renewal, attention, or faithful action.

<a id="FE-B0061"></a>
| Search me, God, and know my heart; test me and know my anxious thoughts. See if there is any offensive way in me, and lead me in the way everlasting.<br>- Psalm 139:23-24 (NIV) |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0062"></a>
| Then, because so many people were coming and going that they did not even have a chance to eat, he said to them, "Come with me by yourselves to a quiet place and get some rest."<br>- Mark 6:31 (NIV) |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0063"></a>
| Now this is what the LORD Almighty says: "Give careful thought to your ways. You have planted much, but harvested little. You eat, but never have enough. You drink, but never have your fill. You put on clothes, but are not warm. You earn wages, only to put them in a purse with holes in it." This is what the LORD Almighty says: "Give careful thought to your ways."<br>- Haggai 1:5-7 (NIV) |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0064"></a>
What I notice in these passages

<a id="FE-B0065"></a>
One truth I need to receive or obey

<a id="FE-B0066"></a>
### Prepare Before the Group

<a id="FE-B0067"></a>
Watch the Week 1 author teaching. Then answer these questions before the group meets. Question numbering restarts each week so your notes remain easy to reference.

<a id="FE-B0068"></a>
1.  What kind of pace am I actually keeping, and what evidence supports my answer?

<a id="FE-B0069"></a>
Possible starting points: a rushed or steady pace; back-to-back commitments; little margin; difficulty slowing down; being present physically but distracted mentally.

<a id="FE-B0070"></a>
2.  Which Energy Audit result surprised me or confirmed something I already knew?

<a id="FE-B0071"></a>
Possible starting points: a lower physical score than expected; strong spiritual practices but weak recovery; emotional depletion hidden by high productivity.

<a id="FE-B0072"></a>
3.  Where am I using willpower, adrenaline, or urgency to cover a lack of renewal?

<a id="FE-B0073"></a>
Possible starting points: pushing through on caffeine; waiting for a deadline to create motivation; treating every request as urgent; skipping recovery because others are counting on me.

<a id="FE-B0074"></a>
WEEK 1 REFLECTION - CONTINUED

<a id="FE-B0075"></a>

<a id="FE-B0076"></a>

<a id="FE-B0078"></a>
Possible starting points: late-night scrolling; saying yes without checking the calendar; unresolved conflict; constant notifications; a commitment that no longer fits this season.

<a id="FE-B0090"></a>
#### Additional Notes

<a id="FE-B0091"></a>
| WEEK 2  &#124;  SPIRITUAL ENERGY<br>Return to the Source<br>Renew spiritual energy |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0092"></a>
| THE BIG IDEA<br>Spiritual energy is not manufactured by trying harder; it is renewed by abiding in God. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0093"></a>
This Week's Outcome

<a id="FE-B0094"></a>
Recognize spiritual dryness, reconnect identity and calling to Christ, and establish a daily return.

<a id="FE-B0095"></a>
Read Before We Meet

<a id="FE-B0096"></a>
The Stewardship Blueprint begins with Bedrock: faith and identity. Spiritual energy is therefore more than one equal battery among four. It is connection to the Source that orders every other source. Jesus' picture of the vine and branches confronts self-reliance: lasting fruit grows from abiding, not frantic effort.

<a id="FE-B0097"></a>
Many men approach God only after depletion has become obvious. We ask for enough strength to continue the pace we selected, while avoiding the deeper question of whether the pace, priorities, and pressure are aligned with Him. Returning to the Source means receiving identity, releasing control, listening for truth, and responding in obedience.

<a id="FE-B0098"></a>
Jesus withdrew to pray and then returned to people and mission. Solitude was not escape from responsibility; it prepared Him to carry responsibility faithfully. A daily return does not need to be impressive. It needs a reliable cue, honest presence, Scripture received as truth, prayer, and one faithful response.

<a id="FE-B0099"></a>

<a id="FE-B0109"></a>

<a id="FE-B0110"></a>

<a id="FE-B0111"></a>

<a id="FE-B0112"></a>
3.  What helps me become present to God instead of merely completing a religious task?

<a id="FE-B0113"></a>
Possible starting points: walking outside without my phone; praying honestly instead of impressively; journaling one verse; worship; silence; talking with a trusted friend or pastor.

<a id="FE-B0114"></a>

<a id="FE-B0117"></a>

<a id="FE-B0118"></a>

#### Additional Notes

<a id="FE-B0131"></a>
| WEEK 3  &#124;  PHYSICAL ENERGY<br>Restore the Body<br>Steward physical capacity |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0132"></a>
| THE BIG IDEA<br>Your body is not the purpose of your life, but it carries you into every purpose God has entrusted to you. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0133"></a>
This Week's Outcome

<a id="FE-B0134"></a>
Treat sleep, movement, nourishment, and recovery as stewardship rather than leftovers.

<a id="FE-B0135"></a>
Read Before We Meet

<a id="FE-B0136"></a>
In 1 Kings 19, Elijah reaches a point of fear, isolation, and collapse. God's first response is not a lecture. Elijah sleeps, eats, drinks, and rests again. God ministers to the whole person. Physical renewal is neither vanity nor the highest good; it is wise care for the body through which you worship, work, love, serve, and endure.

<a id="FE-B0137"></a>
Healthy capacity follows a rhythm of exertion and recovery. Constant strain depletes; constant comfort weakens. The goal is not maximum ease but sustainable faithfulness. Sleep, movement, nourishment, medical wisdom, and Sabbath are not prizes earned after all work is finished. They are part of receiving creaturely limits as gifts.

<a id="FE-B0138"></a>
Physical stewardship must fit your real season and health. Comparison is rarely useful. The strongest next step may be a consistent bedtime, a daily walk, planned training, better fuel, medical follow-up, or protected Sabbath. Choose the rhythm with the greatest likely effect on your presence, patience, clarity, and ability to serve.

<a id="FE-B0139"></a>

<a id="FE-B0170"></a>
#### Additional Notes

<a id="FE-B0171"></a>
| WEEK 4  &#124;  EMOTIONAL ENERGY<br>Guard the Heart<br>Steward emotional energy |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0172"></a>
| THE BIG IDEA<br>Emotional strength is not the absence of feeling; it is the ability to name, process, and direct what you feel. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0173"></a>
This Week's Outcome

<a id="FE-B0174"></a>
Notice emotional withdrawals, process pressure honestly, and change the tone brought into relationships.

<a id="FE-B0175"></a>
Read Before We Meet

<a id="FE-B0176"></a>
Men often confuse emotional control with emotional silence. Scripture is more honest. The Psalms name fear, grief, anger, hope, and trust before God. Emotions are signals, not masters. They can reveal loss, threat, desire, fatigue, or a violated value, but they still need truth and wise direction.

<a id="FE-B0177"></a>
Depletion often changes relational tone before it changes output. Irritability, withdrawal, control, numbness, defensiveness, and cynicism may be warnings that emotional reserves are low or that something important remains unprocessed. Guarding the heart is not hardening it. It is noticing what is happening within you and choosing what you will carry into the next interaction.

<a id="FE-B0178"></a>
A useful practice is Name-Reframe-Respond. Name the emotion and its apparent cause without judgment. Reframe by separating facts, assumptions, and what is within your responsibility. Respond with the next faithful action rather than the fastest reaction. When depleted behavior harms someone, repair quickly and take ownership without defending the impact.

<a id="FE-B0179"></a>

<a id="FE-B0211"></a>
#### Additional Notes

<a id="FE-B0212"></a>
| WEEK 5  &#124;  MENTAL ENERGY<br>Renew the Mind<br>Protect focus and clarity |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0213"></a>
| THE BIG IDEA<br>Attention is directed energy; what repeatedly receives it is what your life is quietly building. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0214"></a>
This Week's Outcome

<a id="FE-B0215"></a>
Reduce fragmentation, renew thought patterns, and protect attention for what matters most.

<a id="FE-B0216"></a>
Read Before We Meet

<a id="FE-B0217"></a>
Your attention is finite. Every alert, open loop, media input, and unresolved decision competes for it. Mental fatigue is not always caused by difficult work; it is often caused by repeated switching and unbounded access. Romans 12 connects transformation to a renewed mind, not merely a more efficient day.

<a id="FE-B0218"></a>
Focus is a form of love. It allows a person, task, prayer, or decision to receive your full presence. Fragmentation gives many things a small portion of you while allowing almost nothing to receive your best. Guardrails reduce the number of choices required in the moment and make focused presence easier.

<a id="FE-B0219"></a>
Mental renewal includes both attention and thought. Recurring thoughts should be examined rather than endlessly rehearsed. Capture the thought, identify the claim it is making, compare that claim with what is true, and choose the next faithful action. The goal is not thought suppression but obedient direction.

<a id="FE-B0220"></a>

<a id="FE-B0231"></a>

<a id="FE-B0233"></a>

<a id="FE-B0252"></a>
#### Additional Notes

<a id="FE-B0253"></a>
| WEEK 6  &#124;  INTEGRATED ENERGY<br>Build the Rhythm<br>Live a sustainable rule of life |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0254"></a>
| THE BIG IDEA<br>Renewal lasts when faithful choices become protected rhythms, not occasional rescue attempts. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0255"></a>
This Week's Outcome

<a id="FE-B0256"></a>
Integrate all four energies into an Energy Rule of Life and commit to the next 30 days.

<a id="FE-B0257"></a>
Read Before We Meet

<a id="FE-B0258"></a>
Rescue attempts fade; rhythms endure. An Energy Rule of Life is not a rigid spiritual scorecard. It is a prayerful structure that helps your calendar reflect your deepest commitments. It must fit the season you are actually in and remain flexible enough to serve love rather than replace it.

<a id="FE-B0259"></a>
Your plan integrates physical, emotional, mental, and spiritual energy while keeping faith and identity as Bedrock. Do not attempt twelve new habits. Choose one primary area for the next 30 days, then keep the others visible through small minimum rhythms. Add guardrails for the predictable conditions under which those rhythms disappear.

<a id="FE-B0260"></a>
A rhythm becomes more durable when another person can see it. Schedule accountability before enthusiasm fades. Retake the Energy Audit, compare direction rather than demanding perfection, and review the plan after 30 days. The goal is not constant high energy. It is faithful availability across work, rest, relationships, and calling.

<a id="FE-B0261"></a>
### Scripture for Week 6

<a id="FE-B0262"></a>
Read each passage slowly. Notice what it reveals about God, human limits, renewal, attention, or faithful action.

<a id="FE-B0263"></a>
| Now when Daniel learned that the decree had been published, he went home to his upstairs room where the windows opened toward Jerusalem. Three times a day he got down on his knees and prayed, giving thanks to his God, just as he had done before.<br>- Daniel 6:10 (NIV) |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0264"></a>
| Very early in the morning, while it was still dark, Jesus got up, left the house and went off to a solitary place, where he prayed.<br>- Mark 1:35 (NIV) |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0265"></a>
| And let us consider how we may spur one another on toward love and good deeds, not giving up meeting together, as some are in the habit of doing, but encouraging one another-and all the more as you see the Day approaching.<br>- Hebrews 10:24-25 (NIV) |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0266"></a>
| Let us not become weary in doing good, for at the proper time we will reap a harvest if we do not give up.<br>- Galatians 6:9 (NIV) |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0267"></a>
What I notice in these passages

<a id="FE-B0268"></a>
One truth I need to receive or obey

<a id="FE-B0269"></a>
### Prepare Before the Group

<a id="FE-B0270"></a>
Watch the Week 6 author teaching. Then answer these questions before the group meets. Question numbering restarts each week so your notes remain easy to reference.

<a id="FE-B0271"></a>
1.  What changed in my awareness, choices, relationships, or capacity during these six weeks?

<a id="FE-B0272"></a>
Possible starting points: I notice depletion earlier; pause before reacting; sleep more consistently; pray more honestly; protect focus; repair faster; ask for help sooner.

<a id="FE-B0273"></a>
2.  Which rhythm produced the greatest return across more than one energy source?

<a id="FE-B0274"></a>
Possible starting points: a consistent bedtime may improve patience and focus; morning prayer may shape emotional steadiness; exercise may increase clarity and reduce stress.

<a id="FE-B0275"></a>
3.  What must I start, stop, continue, and protect?

<a id="FE-B0276"></a>
Possible starting points: start a weekly Sabbath window; stop late-night scrolling; continue morning Scripture; protect a standing workout, date night, or accountability conversation.

<a id="FE-B0277"></a>
WEEK 6 REFLECTION - CONTINUED

<a id="FE-B0278"></a>
4.  What season am I in, and what does sustainable faithfulness look like now, not in an imaginary life?

<a id="FE-B0279"></a>
Possible starting points: a launch season, caregiving season, grief, recovery, parenting transition, demanding work cycle, or rebuilding season; faithfulness may require different rhythms in each.

<a id="FE-B0280"></a>
5.  Who will help me review this plan in 30 and 90 days?

<a id="FE-B0281"></a>
Possible starting points: a spouse, accountability partner, trusted friend, pastor, coach, counselor, physician, or group member who will ask specific questions and expect an honest answer.

<a id="FE-B0282"></a>
### Practice Between Meetings

<a id="FE-B0283"></a>
Retake the Energy Audit before the final meeting.

<a id="FE-B0284"></a>
Choose one keystone rhythm for each energy source, but one primary focus for the next 30 days.

<a id="FE-B0285"></a>
Write two guardrails that protect renewal when pressure rises.

<a id="FE-B0286"></a>
Schedule a 30-day review with an accountability partner and a 90-day audit.

<a id="FE-B0287"></a>
My one commitment this week - what I will do, when, and minimum version

<a id="FE-B0288"></a>
Who will know, and when I will check in

<a id="FE-B0289"></a>
#### End-of-Week Review

<a id="FE-B0290"></a>
What I practiced and what actually happened

<a id="FE-B0291"></a>
What I learned about God, myself, or my current season

<a id="FE-B0292"></a>
| PRAYER<br>Father, help me build rhythms that keep me available to love You, serve people, and carry my calling faithfully. |
| --- |

<a id="FE-B0293"></a>
#### Additional Notes

<a id="FE-B0294"></a>
### My 30-Day Energy Stewardship Plan

<a id="FE-B0295"></a>
Keep this plan realistic. Choose one primary energy source for concentrated attention. Keep the other three visible through small faithful rhythms. Protect the plan with guardrails and people.

<a id="FE-B0308"></a>
### Final Commission

<a id="FE-B0309"></a>
A fully engaged life is not a life of constant intensity. It is a life increasingly aligned with God, available to people, and capable of carrying what matters. Protect what renews you-not to make your life smaller, but so that your love, service, leadership, and legacy can remain faithful.

<a id="FE-B0310"></a>
| REMEMBER<br>You were not created to run on empty. You were created to be fully engaged in a life that matters. |

<a id="BRAND-B0001"></a>
STEWARDSHIP BLUEPRINT

<a id="BRAND-B0002"></a>
Master Branding Document

<a id="BRAND-B0003"></a>
Brand Architecture, Naming Rules, Messaging, and Church/Group Packaging

<a id="BRAND-B0004"></a>
Draft v1 | June 24, 2026

<a id="BRAND-B0005"></a>
| Core Brand Sentence<br>Stewardship Blueprint is the framework. The Life You're Building is the book that teaches it. ForgedLife is the accountability app that helps people live it. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0006"></a>
Purpose of this document

<a id="BRAND-B0007"></a>
This resource establishes the brand architecture for the Stewardship Blueprint ecosystem so future books, studies, church resources, app challenges, small groups, retreats, assessments, and campaigns use consistent language and avoid brand confusion.

<a id="BRAND-B0008"></a>
### 1. Executive Summary

<a id="BRAND-B0009"></a>
Stewardship Blueprint is the core framework, theology, architecture, and long-term movement. It is the operating system for whole-life stewardship discipleship.

<a id="BRAND-B0010"></a>
The Life You're Building is the flagship book and public invitation. It introduces the central question - "What kind of life am I actually building?" - and teaches the Stewardship Blueprint through story, Scripture, reflection, and practical action.

<a id="BRAND-B0011"></a>
ForgedLife is the accountability platform that helps people live the framework. It supports habits, challenges, prayer, group accountability, and practical next steps.

<a id="BRAND-B0012"></a>
| Strategic Principle<br>The ecosystem should never feel like three competing brands. It should feel like one integrated pathway: framework, book, and accountability tool working together for Kingdom impact and life transformation. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0013"></a>
#### The Simplest Explanation

<a id="BRAND-B0014"></a>
Stewardship Blueprint: the framework.

<a id="BRAND-B0015"></a>
The Life You're Building: the book that teaches the framework.

<a id="BRAND-B0016"></a>
ForgedLife: the app that helps people live the framework.

<a id="BRAND-B0017"></a>
### 2. Brand Architecture

<a id="BRAND-B0018"></a>
| Brand Level | Role |
| --- | --- |
| Master Framework | Stewardship Blueprint - the core theology, structure, framework, curriculum spine, and movement. |
| Flagship Book | The Life You're Building: How to Stop Drifting and Design a Life That Matters - the public invitation and teaching narrative. |
| Accountability Platform | ForgedLife - the app/platform that activates the framework through habits, challenges, prayer, and accountability. |
| Assessment Tool | Created for More Check-In - the front-door reflection tool that helps people identify where they are aligned and where they are drifting. |
| Church/Group Experiences | Stewardship Blueprint experiences - sermon series, men's challenges, small groups, retreats, workshops, and discipleship pathways. |
| Men's Track | The Life You're Building Men's Challenge - a Stewardship Blueprint experience powered by ForgedLife. |

<a id="BRAND-B0019"></a>
#### Brand Priority Rule

<a id="BRAND-B0020"></a>
When talking about the overall movement, lead with Stewardship Blueprint.

<a id="BRAND-B0021"></a>
When inviting general audiences or book readers, lead with The Life You're Building.

<a id="BRAND-B0022"></a>
When talking about accountability, habits, or digital challenges, lead with ForgedLife.

<a id="BRAND-B0023"></a>
When talking to churches, describe the offering as a Stewardship Blueprint discipleship pathway, often introduced through The Life You're Building and supported by ForgedLife.

<a id="BRAND-B0024"></a>
### 3. Core Positioning

<a id="BRAND-B0025"></a>
#### Master Positioning Statement

<a id="BRAND-B0026"></a>
| Stewardship Blueprint is a Kingdom-centered, whole-life stewardship framework that helps people stop drifting and faithfully steward what God has entrusted to them - their faith, relationships, health, joy, money, habits, energy, influence, and legacy. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0027"></a>
#### Church-Facing Positioning

<a id="BRAND-B0028"></a>
| The Stewardship Blueprint is a whole-life discipleship pathway that helps people move from drift to design by aligning their faith, relationships, health, joy, resources, habits, energy, and legacy around what God has entrusted to them. The book teaches the framework, groups create conversation, and ForgedLife helps people actually live it through accountability. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0029"></a>
#### Men's Ministry Positioning

<a id="BRAND-B0030"></a>
| The Life You're Building Men's Challenge is a 6-week Stewardship Blueprint experience powered by ForgedLife that helps men wake up, examine the life they are building, integrate faith into every area, build guardrails and habits, and live with brotherhood, accountability, purpose, and Kingdom impact. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0031"></a>
### 4. Messaging Pillars

<a id="BRAND-B0032"></a>
| Pillar | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kingdom Impact | The motive is to help people follow Jesus more intentionally, steward life faithfully, and multiply impact for God's Kingdom. |
| Whole-Life Stewardship | Stewardship includes faith, relationships, health, joy, money, habits, energy, guardrails, influence, and legacy. |
| Drift to Design | Most people do not intentionally waste their lives; they drift. The framework helps them wake up and build with wisdom. |
| Faith Integration | Faith is not one room in the house. Faith is the center that shapes the whole structure. |
| Practical Formation | The goal is not more information. The goal is formation: reflection, conversation, habits, guardrails, accountability, and next steps. |
| Brotherhood and Accountability | Transformation deepens when people are honest, encouraged, challenged, prayed for, and held accountable in community. |
| Legacy and Multiplication | Legacy is not only what is left at death. It is what life multiplies while we are still living. |

<a id="BRAND-B0033"></a>
### 5. Approved Naming Structure

<a id="BRAND-B0034"></a>
Use this naming convention for most studies, campaigns, retreats, and app-connected experiences:

<a id="BRAND-B0035"></a>
| Default Naming Formula<br>[Experience Name]<br>A Stewardship Blueprint Experience<br>Powered by ForgedLife |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0036"></a>
#### Examples

<a id="BRAND-B0037"></a>
| Name | Descriptor |
| --- | --- |
| The Life You're Building Men's Challenge | A 6-week Stewardship Blueprint experience powered by ForgedLife. |
| Created for More Check-In | A Stewardship Blueprint assessment. |
| Guardrails Challenge | A Stewardship Blueprint challenge powered by ForgedLife. |
| Legacy &amp; Impact Journey | A Stewardship Blueprint experience for families, couples, and leaders. |
| The Life You're Building Church Campaign | A church-wide Stewardship Blueprint journey. |
| The Life You're Building Group Guide | A Stewardship Blueprint study for small groups. |

<a id="BRAND-B0038"></a>
#### When to Lead with Each Brand

<a id="BRAND-B0039"></a>
| Brand Lead | Use Case |
| --- | --- |
| Lead with Stewardship Blueprint | When explaining the larger framework, theology, movement, church pathway, or discipleship architecture. |
| Lead with The Life You're Building | When promoting the book, a sermon series, a public-facing campaign, or an invitation for people to ask what kind of life they are building. |
| Lead with ForgedLife | When promoting app-based accountability, men's challenges, habit tracking, brotherhood, and digital group follow-through. |

<a id="BRAND-B0040"></a>
### 6. Product and Experience Map

<a id="BRAND-B0041"></a>
| Offering | Type | Primary Function |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Stewardship Blueprint | Master framework and movement. | Whole-life stewardship discipleship. |
| The Life You're Building | Flagship book. | Explains the framework and invites readers to stop drifting and design a life that matters. |
| Created for More Check-In | Assessment. | Helps people see where they are aligned, drifting, and what needs attention next. |
| The Life You're Building Men's Challenge | 6-week men's challenge. | A Core5/church men's pilot powered by ForgedLife accountability. |
| Stewardship Blueprint Group Experience | Small group curriculum. | Discussion, reflection, Scripture, action steps, and practical application. |
| Stewardship Blueprint Church Campaign | Church-wide pathway. | Sermon series, groups, app challenge, assessment, and next-step discipleship. |
| ForgedLife | Accountability app/platform. | Keeps people accountable to the teachings through habits, challenges, prayer, and group rhythms. |
| Legacy &amp; Impact Journey | Family/couples/leader experience. | Helps people clarify values, generosity, family formation, influence, and legacy. |

<a id="BRAND-B0042"></a>
### 7. The Core Framework Language

<a id="BRAND-B0043"></a>
#### Recommended Framework Description

<a id="BRAND-B0044"></a>
| The Stewardship Blueprint helps people see life as something entrusted by God and intentionally build around faith, purpose, relationships, health, joy, resources, guardrails, habits, energy, and legacy. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0045"></a>
#### Expanded Framework Description

<a id="BRAND-B0046"></a>
The Stewardship Blueprint is a whole-life framework for faithful living. It helps people awaken from drift, see the structure of their life, place faith at the center, evaluate the core pillars of relationships, health, joy, and resources, build guardrails before regret, establish keystone habits, steward energy, and multiply Kingdom impact through legacy, generosity, and influence.

<a id="BRAND-B0047"></a>
#### Framework Layers

<a id="BRAND-B0048"></a>
| Layer | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Bedrock | Faith - what life is rooted in. |
| Foundation | Purpose, values, calling, mission, and direction. |
| Pillars | Family/relationships, fitness/health, fun/joy, and finances/resources. |
| Guardrails | Boundaries that protect what matters before regret arrives. |
| Keystone Habits | Small repeated rhythms that strengthen the structure. |
| Energy | The fuel behind faithful stewardship: spiritual, physical, mental, and emotional capacity. |
| Legacy | What life multiplies through people, generosity, influence, and Kingdom impact. |

<a id="BRAND-B0049"></a>
### 8. Church and Ministry Packaging

<a id="BRAND-B0050"></a>
#### Primary Church Offer

<a id="BRAND-B0051"></a>

| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0052"></a>

4-6 week sermon series.

Men's challenge or cohort.

Church-wide stewardship campaign.

Leadership retreat or elder/deacon/staff formation pathway.

Couples/family legacy workshop.

#### Core5 Pilot Name

<a id="BRAND-B0061"></a>

| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0062"></a>
#### Core5 Pilot Purpose

<a id="BRAND-B0063"></a>
Use Core5 as the first trusted testing environment to refine the content, weekly rhythm, app challenge, discussion questions, language, Scriptures, accountability prompts, and transformation outcomes before packaging for broader men's groups or church use.

<a id="BRAND-B0064"></a>
### 9. The 6-Week Challenge Framework

<a id="BRAND-B0065"></a>
| Week | Theme | Core Question | Key Scripture |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Week 1 - Wake Up | Stop drifting long enough to examine the life being built. | What kind of life am I actually building? | Psalm 90:12 |
| Week 2 - See the Blueprint | Understand the structure of a faithful life. | What is my life rooted in and built around? | Matthew 7:24-25 |
| Week 3 - Evaluate the Pillars | Examine the core areas entrusted to us: relationships, health, joy, and resources. | Where am I aligned, and where am I drifting? | 1 Peter 4:10 |
| Week 4 - Build Guardrails | Establish boundaries before regret arrives. | Where am I vulnerable, and what guardrail would protect what matters? | Proverbs 4:23 |
| Week 5 - Strengthen the Structure | Identify keystone habits and energy rhythms. | What small rhythm would pull the rest of my life toward alignment? | Galatians 6:9 |
| Week 6 - Multiply the Impact | Clarify legacy, brotherhood, and next faithful steps. | What will my life multiply, and who will help me stay accountable? | Matthew 5:16 |

<a id="BRAND-B0066"></a>
### 10. Voice, Tone, and Theological Posture

<a id="BRAND-B0067"></a>
#### Voice

<a id="BRAND-B0068"></a>
Pastoral, clear, practical, and invitational.

<a id="BRAND-B0069"></a>
Biblically rooted without becoming churchy or abstract.

<a id="BRAND-B0070"></a>
Convicting without shame.

<a id="BRAND-B0071"></a>
Action-oriented without sounding like self-help or performance spirituality.

<a id="BRAND-B0072"></a>
Honest about drift while consistently pointing toward grace, wisdom, and transformation.

<a id="BRAND-B0073"></a>
#### Theological Posture

<a id="BRAND-B0074"></a>
God is the owner and giver of life; we are stewards.

<a id="BRAND-B0075"></a>
Faith is the center that integrates the whole life.

<a id="BRAND-B0076"></a>
The goal is not optimization for personal success; the goal is faithful stewardship for Kingdom impact.

<a id="BRAND-B0077"></a>
Transformation is not merely behavior change. It is discipleship, surrender, wisdom, accountability, and Spirit-led formation.

<a id="BRAND-B0078"></a>
Stewardship includes money, but it is much larger than money.

<a id="BRAND-B0079"></a>
#### Language to Prefer

<a id="BRAND-B0080"></a>
Whole-life stewardship.

<a id="BRAND-B0081"></a>
Faithful life.

<a id="BRAND-B0082"></a>
Kingdom impact.

<a id="BRAND-B0083"></a>
Move from drift to design.

<a id="BRAND-B0084"></a>
What God has entrusted.

<a id="BRAND-B0085"></a>
Build a life that matters.

<a id="BRAND-B0086"></a>
Faith integrated into the actual structure of life.

<a id="BRAND-B0087"></a>
Brotherhood, accountability, purpose, and formation.

<a id="BRAND-B0088"></a>
#### Language to Avoid or Use Carefully

<a id="BRAND-B0089"></a>
Do not imply that financial sales, SIG, FIS, or advisory opportunities are the motive or primary driver.

<a id="BRAND-B0090"></a>
Do not over-frame the ministry as something it is not. Lead positively with what it is: a Kingdom-centered, whole-life stewardship framework.

<a id="BRAND-B0091"></a>
Avoid making ForgedLife sound like the master brand for all audiences; it is the accountability layer, especially strong for men.

<a id="BRAND-B0092"></a>
Avoid using too many brand names in one sentence unless explaining the architecture clearly.

<a id="BRAND-B0093"></a>
Use "best life" carefully in church contexts; often prefer faithful life, fruitful life, life God entrusted, or life that matters.

<a id="BRAND-B0094"></a>
### 11. Elevator Pitches

<a id="BRAND-B0095"></a>
#### 10-Second Pitch

<a id="BRAND-B0096"></a>
| Stewardship Blueprint helps people stop drifting and faithfully steward the life God has entrusted to them. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0097"></a>
#### 30-Second Pitch

<a id="BRAND-B0098"></a>
| Stewardship Blueprint is a whole-life discipleship framework that helps people align their faith, relationships, health, joy, resources, habits, energy, and legacy around what God has entrusted to them. The Life You're Building teaches the framework, groups create the conversation, and ForgedLife helps people actually live it through accountability. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0099"></a>
#### Church Leader Pitch

<a id="BRAND-B0100"></a>
| The Life You're Building Church Campaign is a Stewardship Blueprint pathway that helps churches guide people from drift to faithful stewardship. It can be used as a sermon series, small group curriculum, men's challenge, or digital accountability journey powered by ForgedLife. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0101"></a>
#### Men's Group Pitch

<a id="BRAND-B0102"></a>
| The Life You're Building Men's Challenge is a 6-week Stewardship Blueprint experience that helps men wake up, evaluate the life they are building, strengthen faith, relationships, health, joy, and resources, build guardrails and habits, and live with brotherhood and accountability through ForgedLife. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0103"></a>
### 12. Brand Governance Rules

<a id="BRAND-B0104"></a>
Stewardship Blueprint remains the core framework and master brand.

<a id="BRAND-B0105"></a>
The Life You're Building remains the flagship book and public invitation.

<a id="BRAND-B0106"></a>
ForgedLife remains the accountability platform, not the full framework.

<a id="BRAND-B0107"></a>
Every study, challenge, or campaign should identify its relationship to the Stewardship Blueprint.

<a id="BRAND-B0108"></a>
When multiple brands appear together, use the sentence: "The book teaches the framework, the group creates conversation, and the app helps people live it."

<a id="BRAND-B0109"></a>
Church-facing materials should emphasize Kingdom impact, discipleship, spiritual formation, and whole-life stewardship.

<a id="BRAND-B0110"></a>
Men's ministry materials may use stronger language around brotherhood, courage, accountability, discipline, and legacy.

<a id="BRAND-B0111"></a>
Future resources should use consistent descriptors: "A Stewardship Blueprint Experience," "A Stewardship Blueprint Challenge," "A Stewardship Blueprint Assessment," or "Powered by ForgedLife."

<a id="BRAND-B0112"></a>
Protect the movement from becoming overly tied to one product, one book, one app, one audience, or one ministry lane.

<a id="BRAND-B0113"></a>
The ultimate measure of brand clarity is whether someone can explain the ecosystem in one sentence without confusion.

<a id="BRAND-B0114"></a>
### 13. Future Brand Extensions

<a id="BRAND-B0115"></a>
| Extension | Potential Use |
| --- | --- |
| Stewardship Blueprint for Men | Men's discipleship and accountability pathway. |
| Stewardship Blueprint for Couples | Marriage, family, rhythms, communication, purpose, and shared legacy. |
| Stewardship Blueprint for Families | Family values, next-generation formation, rhythms, money conversations, and legacy. |

| Created for More Check-In | Entry-level assessment and lead-in to deeper experiences. |

<a id="BRAND-B0116"></a>
### 14. One-Sentence Reference

<a id="BRAND-B0117"></a>
| Use This Often<br>Stewardship Blueprint is the core framework for whole-life discipleship; The Life You're Building is the flagship book that teaches it; and ForgedLife is the accountability app that helps people live it. |
| --- |

<a id="BRAND-B0118"></a>
### 15. Final Brand Clarity Test

<a id="BRAND-B0119"></a>
Before naming any new resource, study, challenge, app feature, church campaign, or event, ask:

<a id="BRAND-B0120"></a>
Does this clearly connect back to the Stewardship Blueprint framework?

<a id="BRAND-B0121"></a>
Does the name make sense without explaining all three brands at once?

<a id="BRAND-B0122"></a>
Is The Life You're Building being used as the book/campaign hook rather than competing with the framework?

<a id="BRAND-B0123"></a>
Is ForgedLife being used as the accountability layer rather than the entire movement?

<a id="BRAND-B0124"></a>
